# NoorHeroes — Consolidated Execution Blueprint

**Single source of truth for build order across Backend (FastAPI), KMP/Compose Multiplatform (Android/iOS), Next.js Admin & Scholar Portal, the Parent Web Account Portal, shared/core modules, infrastructure, and AI/GenAI microservices.**

Generated: 2026-08-19 · Updated: 2026-09-25 (cross-verified against Repo Foundation documentation, implementation codebase, and CI test suites: 5,817 backend tests passing across 390 test modules with zero failures, and 361 KMP host unit tests passing across all 16 core/library modules) · Source corpus: `NoorHeroes_Consolidated_Blueprint_v6.md`, `NoorHeroes_Final_Development_Prompt.md`, `NoorHeroes_Feature_reference.md`, 37 `Development_Blueprints/` modules (678 implementation files across Backend/KMP/NextJS_Admin/Parent_Web_Portal), 37 `PRD_Documents/Feature_*.md` files, and 16 `Core_Setup_Docs/` architecture-instruction manuals documenting the already-implemented Python FastAPI, KMP App, and NextJS Admin Web foundations.

---

## 0. How to Use This Document

This blueprint sequences **every implementation file already defined in the NoorHeroes corpus**, plus every implementation file the corpus is missing, into one dependency-ordered build plan. It is organized into 13 phases (Phase 0–12). Within each phase, work is grouped by feature/module (matching the existing `Development_Blueprints/<Module>/` folder names so every entry stays traceable to its source PRD and blueprint file); within each module, files are listed Backend → KMP → NextJS_Admin, because that is the order the existing blueprints themselves assume (KMP and NextJS_Admin files declare the Backend endpoints they call; Backend files declare, where documented, which KMP/NextJS blueprints consume them).

Each row in a module table gives: a **global sequence number** (position in the whole 700+ file build order), the file's existing **Blueprint ID** (`B-NN`/`K-NN`/`N-NN`, already unique within its module+platform), the **recommended filename** in the required dotted convention (`Development_Blueprints.<Module>.<Platform>.<NN>-<slug>.md` — mechanically derived from each file's real path, so every filename below is exactly what exists on disk today, just re-rendered), the **platform**, a short **purpose** synopsis (drawn from the file's own `## 4. Objective` section), what it **depends on**, and its **status** (`Existing` = already fully specified in the corpus and ready to hand to an implementation agent as-is; `NEW` = does not exist and is defined for the first time in §14 of this document, with its own rationale, exact filename, and insertion point).

Sections at the end — §14 Dependency & Order Validation, §15 Missing Blueprint Files, §16 Final Execution Checklist — are the parts a tech lead should read first if short on time; §1–§13 are the phase-by-phase execution detail a dev team works through top to bottom. **New readers should start with §0.5 below**, which summarizes what the team has already built and verified against this blueprint before diving into phase detail.

  - **B-03 (Admin Avatar Item Catalog Management API):** Administrative CRUD and governance surface for `avatar_items`: endpoints `POST /v1/admin/avatar-items` (create with structural unlock rule validation & audit logging), `GET /v1/admin/avatar-items` (paginated catalog listing with `slot` and `is_retired` filtering), `GET /v1/admin/avatar-items/{itemId}` (single item detail with resolved `unlock_rule_description`), `PATCH /v1/admin/avatar-items/{itemId}` (non-retroactive forward-only metadata/criteria update), `DELETE /v1/admin/avatar-items/{itemId}` (soft-retires item via `is_retired = True`; hard delete strictly forbidden); cross-context progression rule validation via `UnlockRuleValidator` with discriminated union `UnlockRuleUnion` (`BadgeUnlockRule`, `LevelUnlockRule`, `HabitMilestoneUnlockRule`) validating referenced `badge_id` in `rewards.badges`, level bounds, and canonical habit milestones (`CANONICAL_HABIT_MILESTONES`); P0 retire-not-delete & child grandfathering guarantee (retired items hidden from discovery but permanently equippable by existing owners per PRD FR-10 / Edge Case 11); zero monetization invariant; RBAC gating (`content_editor`); and atomic `AdminAuditRecord` logging with before/after state diffs.
  - **B-04 (Admin Avatar Base Library API):** Administrative CRUD endpoints for base character library (`/v1/admin/avatars` & `/admin/v1/avatars`): endpoints `POST /v1/admin/avatars` (create new base avatar character with strict name/asset uniqueness rejecting duplicates with HTTP 409 Conflict), `GET /v1/admin/avatars` (paginated listing with `selectable` filter and case-insensitive search), `GET /v1/admin/avatars/{avatarId}` (single base avatar detail), `PATCH /v1/admin/avatars/{avatarId}` (partial update with conflict re-verification); deliberate absence of DELETE route (P0 foreign key invariant protecting `ChildProfile.avatar_id` references, disabling achieved exclusively via `selectable=False`, guarded by non-bypassable route-introspection test); dual-router mounting (`/v1/admin/avatars` and direct `/admin/v1/avatars`) with dual-wire camelCase/snake_case support; RBAC gating (`admin_read` for GET, `content_editor` for mutations); and atomic `AdminAuditRecord` logging.
  - **B-05 (Modesty & Appropriateness Review Workflow API):** Cultural modesty and appropriateness review workflow for avatars and avatar items: endpoints `POST /v1/admin/avatars/review/{id}/submit` & `POST /v1/admin/avatar-items/review/{id}/submit` (submit for review), `POST /v1/admin/avatars/review/{id}/approve` & `POST /v1/admin/avatar-items/review/{id}/approve` (approve and publish), `POST /v1/admin/avatars/review/{id}/reject` & `POST /v1/admin/avatar-items/review/{id}/reject` (reject with mandatory feedback); four-eyes modesty verification & segregation of duties enforcing that the approving admin must differ from the submitting admin (`approving_admin_id != submitting_admin_id`), blocking self-approval at database and service layer (`SelfApprovalProhibitedError` HTTP 422); state transition machine (`draft` -> `pending_review` -> `published` / `rejected`); RBAC enforcement (`modesty_reviewer`, `content_editor`, `superadmin`); dual-router mounting across `/v1/admin/...` and direct `/admin/v1/...` aliases; and atomic audit logging and live catalog/discovery cache purging on status transition.
  - **B-06 (Item Ownership Distribution Analytics API):** Administrative read-only aggregation endpoint `GET /v1/admin/avatar-items/{itemId}/ownership-distribution` (and direct alias `/admin/v1/avatar-items/{itemId}/ownership-distribution`); database-level SQL aggregation (`COUNT(*)` for `owned_count` and `COUNT(CASE WHEN is_equipped THEN 1 END)` for `equipped_count`) across `child_inventory`; privacy-by-design invariant strictly providing aggregate metrics with zero individual child identities, IDs, names, or accounts exposed (PRD §8/§9 Item 3 compliance); Redis caching with short TTL and resilient live DB fallback; RBAC gating (`product_admin`, `content_editor`, `superadmin`); and 4-language i18n localization and Pydantic v2 camelCase/snake_case serialization.
  - **B-07 (Companion Evolution Tier API):** Child companion evolution state machine backing PRD FR-9 and closing K-05 contract assumptions: dedicated table `child_companion_evolution` (Alembic migration `0079_create_child_companion_evolution.py`, PK `child_id`, `tier: Integer` default 0, `tier_name: String(128)`, `last_advanced_at: DateTime`); standalone endpoint `GET /v1/children/{id}/companion-evolution` for lightweight Home Screen companion presentation; extended composite endpoint `GET /v1/children/{id}/inventory` returning `companionEvolution: { tier, tierName, lastAdvancedAt }`; `EvolutionTierRuleEngine` evaluating progressive milestones (Tier 0 baseline, Tier 1 "First Steps", Tier 2 "Dedicated Learner", Tier 3 "Radiant Explorer", Tier 4 "Master Learner") against level, badges, and habit streaks; P0 Monotonicity Guarantee ensuring evolution tiers are strictly monotonic and sticky, never regressing under event replay, out-of-order delivery, or rule modifications (`AvatarRepository.advance_tier_if_higher`); new child safe default returning `tier: 0`, `tierName: "companion.tier.0"`, `lastAdvancedAt: null` (never 404); and strict child session authorization (`CHILD_SESSION_MISMATCH` HTTP 403 on cross-child queries).
- **Phase 6 Features (§6.11 A.2 Home Dashboard & AI Personalization):** Backend Blueprint Items 335 through 342 (`A2_Home_Dashboard_AI_Personalization` B-01 `01-home-aggregation-feed-api.md`, B-02 `02-ai-recommendation-safety-filter-service.md`, B-03 `03-habit-checkin-streak-api.md`, B-04 `04-daily-mission-assignment-engine.md`, B-05 `05-continue-learning-watching-progress-api.md`, B-06 `06-weekly-challenge-milestone-api.md`, B-07 `07-islamic-fact-occasion-calendar-service.md`, and B-08 `08-billing-content-safety-allowlist-audit.md`) are fully **Implemented & Verified** with 100% test pass rate across all dedicated unit, integration, facade, explainability, streak state, cap trigger, and challenges suites:
  - **B-01 (Home Aggregation Feed API):** Implemented dedicated `home` bounded context (`src/noorheroes/modules/home/`) with `HomeAggregationService` exposing `GET /v1/home` (and direct compatibility facade in `app/`), orchestrating concurrent fan-out to 7 upstream service clients (Recommendation, Progress, Habits, Weekly Challenge, Milestones/Achievements, Content Catalog Missions/Quiz, Islamic Calendar/Occasions) via `asyncio.gather(..., return_exceptions=True)` with 400ms SLA timeouts; fixed 10-section response contract in strict literal FR-1 priority order; pure server-side bedtime window evaluation supporting midnight-crossing spans (`20:00` to `06:00`); `is_first_day: true` empty-state detection for zero-activity children; ETag-based conditional caching (`If-None-Match` -> `304 Not Modified`); 5-minute Redis edge caching (`home:{child_id}`) with partial section dirty invalidation (`invalidate_section`) and fail-open DB resilience; strict response-shaping allowlist with Pydantic v2 `extra="forbid"` and recursive anti-billing CI audit ensuring zero subscription/pricing terms leak into child sessions (FR-9); rate limiting profile `home_aggregation` (30 req/min/child); OpenAPI 3.1.0 specifications synchronized in `artifacts/openapi.json` and `FAST-API-INSTRUCTIONS.md` §5.18.
  - **B-02 (AI Recommendation Engine, Safety Filter & Decision Explainability API):** Implemented ranking and safety filter service in `src/noorheroes/modules/home/`: non-bypassable fail-closed `AISafetyFilterClient` with 400ms SLA timeout and deterministic pre-flight anti-billing/commercial token scanning; candidate content scorer and signals (parent restrictions and recent dislikes stripped before scoring, age-band validation, incomplete activity +30, mastery gap +25, prophet stories +20, bedtime calming +35 / -50 quiz penalty, offline availability +15); scholar-curated 4-language evergreen fallback pipeline (PRD Edge Case 2) guaranteeing recommendation tray is never empty; feature flag `home_ai_ranking_enabled` to bypass ML; Child Greeting Service with 4-language phrasing pools, script rotation, safety filtering, and Islamic occasion fallback (Edge Case 8); privacy-preserving decision audit logging (`recommendation_decision_log` table, Alembic migration `0080_create_recommendation_decision_log.py`) strictly persisting SHA-256 hashed `child_id_hash` (zero raw child PII); Admin Recommendation Explainability API (`GET /admin/v1/recommendations/explain` and `GET /v1/admin/recommendations/explain`) supporting server-side hashing, date filtering, pagination, strict read-only enforcement (405 on mutations), sensitive-data-view audit logging (`recommendations.explain_view`), and strict `trust_safety`/`super_admin` RBAC gating.
  - **B-03 (Habit Check-In & Streak API):** Implemented in `habits` bounded context (`POST /v1/habits/check-ins`, `GET /v1/habits/today`): Database-enforced idempotency (`habit_log` with `uq_habit_log_child_habit_date` and `uq_habit_log_client_request_id`), dual-wire payload aliases (`date` for `local_date`, `client_event_id` for `client_request_id`); 200 OK idempotent replay semantics (`was_duplicate=true`) with zero duplicate reward side-effects; pure non-punitive Mercy Day algorithm (`calculate_mercy_day_streak`, `HabitRepository.compute_streak`, `HabitService.compute_streak`) ensuring single missed days within an active streak do not zero it while two consecutive missed days break the streak; immediate Home edge-cache invalidation (`HomeCache().invalidate_section(child_id, 'habits')`); cross-child access rejection with 403 Forbidden; rate-limit profile `habit_checkin` (60 req/min/child); verified across unit and integration suites (`test_habit_service_mercy_day.py` and `test_habit_checkin_idempotency.py`).
  - **B-04 (Daily Mission Assignment Engine & Candidate Curation Tool):** Implemented in `missions` bounded context (`GET /v1/missions/today`, `POST /v1/missions/{assignment_id}/complete`, `GET/POST/PATCH /admin/v1/mission-pool`, Feature A.2 / Blueprint B-04 & Admin N-02): Database-enforced hard-cap BEFORE INSERT trigger (`trg_daily_mission_cap`, Alembic migration `0082_add_mission_cap_trigger.py`) for PostgreSQL and SQLite strictly preventing >3 mission rows per `(child_id, assignment_date)` (PRD FR-4, §5 item 9); `mission_pool_entry` candidate curation table (`0081_create_daily_mission_tables.py`) with soft-delete only (`active=false`, physical hard DELETE returns 405 Method Not Allowed); Algorithm A-Res weighted sampling without replacement from candidate pool filtered by age bands and active date range; idempotent per-day assignments returning existing rows without duplicate generation; in-place completion updating existing row and invalidating `today_mission` edge-cache fragment (`HomeCache().invalidate_section(child_id, 'today_mission')`); cross-child ownership scoping (403 Forbidden); Admin CRUD endpoints with audit logging (`AdminAuditRecord` to `admin_audit_records`); clean modular architecture under `src/noorheroes/modules/missions/` with backwards-compatibility re-export facades under `app/`; 100% test pass rate across `tests/integration/test_mission_cap_trigger.py` (12/12 passing).
  - **B-05 (Continue Learning & Continue Watching Progress Tracking API):** Implemented in `progress` bounded context (`GET /v1/progress/resume`, `POST /v1/progress/record`, Feature A.2 / Blueprint B-05): `progress_record` table (Alembic migration `0083_create_progress_record_table.py`) with bounds check constraint (`0.0 <= furthest_position_percent <= 100.0`) and unique constraint `(child_id, content_id)`; monotonic progression protection in `ProgressRepository` ensuring late-arriving stale updates cannot regress recorded progress; deterministic recency-based sync race resolution (`updated_at DESC`); type segmentation between `continue_learning` (`story`, `quran`, `quiz`, `lesson`) and `continue_watching` (`video`, `animation`, `episode`) with zero cross-type dedup bleed; boundary state exclusion (`0%` unstarted and `100%` completed items); catalog withdrawn content filtering; 10-item rail capping; child session tenancy isolation (403 Forbidden on sibling access); dual-wire camelCase/snake_case wire parity; 100% backwards-compatibility re-export facades under `app/`; and 100% test pass rate across unit (`test_progress_dedup_race.py`, 16/16 passing) and integration (`test_progress_integration.py`, 4/4 passing) test suites.
  - **B-06 (Weekly Challenge & Milestone API):** Implemented in `challenges` bounded context (`GET /v1/challenges/weekly`, `POST /v1/challenges/{challenge_id}/join`, and `GET /v1/children/{id}/achievements`, Feature A.2 / Blueprint B-06): Database layer with `challenge_definition`, `challenge_participation`, `achievement_definition`, and `achievement_unlock` tables (Alembic migration `0084_create_challenge_achievement_tables.py`); active challenge resolution by date window and age band with graceful null fallback when none active; idempotent join mutation guarded by unique DB constraints and edge-cache invalidation; unified milestone achievements endpoint returning 7-day recent unlocks, nearest unmet milestone teaser, and cumulative levels with child session tenant isolation (403 Forbidden); backwards-compatibility re-export facades under `app/`; and 100% test pass rate across unit and integration test suites.
  - **B-07 (Islamic Fact & Occasion Calendar Service):** Implemented in `facts` bounded context (`GET /v1/facts/daily`, Alembic migration `0085_create_home_fact_and_calendar_tables.py`): 30-day child exposure history deduplication (`seen_fact_ids_last_30d`), multi-child history isolation, evergreen fallback for small/exhausted locale catalogs, fail-safe Islamic calendar occasion resolver (`resolve_occasion`) resolving Jummah and occasion mappings across 4 locales (EN, AR, UR, HI) with safe fallback to `None` on calculation exception without crashing greetings, Celery nightly pruning job (`facts.prune_fact_history`), Admin CRUD endpoints with mandatory server-side scholar approval validation (`scholar_status='approved'` before activation), 100% backwards-compatibility facades under `app/`, and 100% test pass rate across 38 dedicated tests.
  - **B-08 (Billing-Content Safety Allowlist Audit & Continuous Rollback Gate):** Implemented in `home` bounded context (`safety_audit_run` table, Alembic migration `0086_create_safety_audit_run_table.py`): Non-skippable CI blocking gate (`.github/workflows/home-safety-audit.yml`) running a 52-test matrix across tiers and locales with canary injection; continuous production sampling audit job (`home.production_safety_sampling_audit`) running hourly with automated emergency rollback (`SafetyAuditService.trigger_emergency_rollback()`); Trust & Safety emergency bypass protocol (`POST /admin/v1/home/safety-audit-runs/{run_id}/override`) with immutable `AdminAuditRecord` logging; read-only paginated audit log API (`GET /admin/v1/home/safety-audit-runs`); operational runbook (`docs/runbooks/billing-content-safety-emergency-bypass.md`); and 100% test pass rate across 52 dedicated safety tests (117 total home regression tests).
- **Phase 6 Features (§6.12 B.13 Search & Discovery):** Backend Blueprint Items 356 through 362 (`B13_Search_Discovery` B-01 `01-child-search-query-api.md`, B-02 `02-content-search-indexing-sync.md`, B-03 `03-voice-search-transcription-service.md`, B-04 `04-recent-search-history-management.md`, B-05 `05-parent-area-search-api.md`, B-06 `06-admin-content-search-api.md`, and B-07 `07-admin-search-operations-monitoring-api.md`) are 100% **Implemented & Verified** with 227/227 dedicated search tests passing (100% green, 0 failures, zero missing scenarios):

  - **B-02 (Content Search Indexing Sync):** Implemented `SearchIndexSyncWorker` in `modules/search/sync_worker.py` consuming `ContentPublished`, `ContentUpdated`, and `ContentWithdrawn` outbox events via Celery tasks (`search.sync_content_published`, `search.sync_content_updated`, `search.sync_content_withdrawn`): Non-negotiable fail-closed rule rejecting content missing valid `age_band_eligibility` and logging to `search_index_rejection` audit table (Alembic migration `0088_create_search_index_rejection_table.py`); soft-flag withdrawal (`is_published=False`) with child search query omission/re-inclusion; idempotent duplicate event deliveries; out-of-order event tolerance; retry with exponential backoff on transient indexing write failures (`retry_with_backoff(idempotent=True)`); non-blocking execution guarantee (`process_event_safe()`) ensuring indexing failures never roll back primary content publication transactions; operational batch catalog reindexing CLI tool (`scripts/reindex_search_catalog.py`); and complete automated verification across 66 dedicated search tests (100% green).

  - **B-04 (Recent Search History Management):** Delivered `GET /v1/search/recent`, `DELETE /v1/search/recent`, and `DELETE /v1/search/recent/{search_id}` under `modules/search/` with `RecentSearchRepository` (`recent_search` table, migration `0089_create_recent_search_table.py`). Features 20-item automatic rolling window capping and pruning, case-insensitive recency bumping, individual item dismissal (`✖`), 100% server-side `child_id` derivation with zero external parameter acceptance (Edge Case 4 sibling isolation), non-blocking background write side-effects on text and voice searches, pre-aggregation content safety filtering via `AISafetyService` (Edge Case 3), and 100% test pass rate across 39 dedicated tests (152 search module tests passing).

  - **B-06 (Admin Content Search API):** Delivered `GET /admin/v1/search` (and canonical alias `GET /v1/admin/search`) under `modules/search/` with `AdminSearchService` and `AdminSearchRepository` querying raw content entities directly with operator filters (`q`, `status`, `review_state`, `language`, `tag`), non-age-restricted visibility, mandatory `AdminAuditRepository` audit logging, RBAC scope enforcement, dual-wire camelCase/snake_case serialization, thin controller discipline, episode cascade suppression, and verified CI-blocking AST architectural isolation from child and parent search modules (34/34 tests passing, 209 search module tests passing).

Enforces distinct RBAC scopes per monitor, strict structural privacy on `voice_search_confidence_log` (zero child_id/transcript/audio columns), fail-closed content safety filtering on zero-result queries (Edge Case 3), 90-day bounded date range validation, dual-wire serialization, read-only 405 contracts, and 100% test pass rate across 18 dedicated monitoring tests and 227 search module tests.

## 3. Phase 2 — Backend Foundation & Security Baseline Prerequisites

**Purpose:** Build the FastAPI Clean-Architecture scaffolding every domain service in every later phase plugs into, then immediately build the non-negotiable security primitives (`F37_Security_Architecture`) that Final_Development_Prompt §9 explicitly calls **prerequisite infrastructure, not a schedulable feature** — before a single feature endpoint (including A1's own auth endpoints) is written. **Entry criteria:** Phase 1 complete. **Exit criteria:** an empty FastAPI app boots against the local Postgres/Redis stack with structlog, correlation-ID middleware, the domain-error hierarchy, and a `conftest.py` + Testcontainers harness all working; the canonical JWT schema, refresh-rotation, credential hashing, and gateway-zero-authz architectural test all pass in CI.

**Completion finding — foundation (updated):** `FAST-API-INSTRUCTIONS.md` (the team's own "Status: Production Ready" developer manual) confirms all 9 items below are already implemented — bounded-context folder layout, `Depends()`-based DI, pydantic-settings config, structlog+correlation IDs, domain-error hierarchy, base repository/service layer, transactional outbox, testing harness, and rate-limit/CORS middleware. These are no longer pending work; kept below (re-labeled ✅) for build-order traceability only. Note item 03's original "sourced from Phase 1 secrets management" dependency and item 04/07's Phase 1 dependencies are therefore satisfied by whatever config/observability the team is currently using locally — full Phase 1 (§2) production-grade environments/observability/CI are still a separate, genuinely open item.

**No gap — security baseline (updated):** `F37_Security_Architecture` is fully specified as 9 existing Backend blueprint files, 1 KMP file, and 1 NextJS_Admin file. 6 of the 9 Backend files are foundational primitives that gate every authenticated endpoint in the system and are pulled forward into this phase. **`B-01` (Canonical JWT Claim Schema), `B-02` (Refresh-Token Rotation & Redis Denylist), `B-03` (API Gateway Zero-Business-Authorization Architecture), `B-04` (Slow-Hash Credential Storage Utility), `B-06` (Domain-Service Independent Child-Scoped Re-Verification Helper), and `B-07` (Multi-Dimensional Rate Limiting Configuration) are fully Implemented & Verified** with the canonical 9-claim `JwtClaims` model, `create_access_token`, child token issuance, `RefreshTokenService`, `ParentSessionRepository`, `RefreshTokenDenylist`, `StructuredLoggingMiddleware` telemetry signal, the canonical `CredentialHasher` utility with OWASP Argon2id cost parameters, the canonical `verify_child_scope()` and `validate_parent_mode_scope()` helpers, `SlidingWindowRateLimiter.check_and_increment_multi` with independent 4-dimension enforcement (device, IP, phone, account), single-roundtrip atomic Redis sorted sets, `docs/architecture/multidimensional-rate-limiting.md`, and the CI-blocking test harnesses `test_gateway_zero_authorization.py`, `test_no_fast_hash_in_credential_path.py`, `test_domain_service_reverification_under_simulated_gateway_bypass.py`, `test_multidimensional_rate_limiting.py`, and `test_rate_limit_bypass_resistance.py`. The remaining Backend security baseline files continue into execution.

## 4. Phase 3 — Shared/Core Modules (KMP `core-*`, Next.js Admin Foundation)

**Purpose:** Build the 12+ KMP `core-*` Gradle modules and the Next.js Admin foundational infrastructure that every `feature/*` module and every Admin page respectively is built on. **Entry criteria:** Phase 2 complete (KMP's `core-network` needs a live backend to integration-test against; `core-security`'s hashing must match Phase 2's F37 B-04 server-side algorithm). **Exit criteria:** Final_Development_Prompt §13.4's Phase 0 exit criteria — a working end-to-end **"hello family"** vertical slice (a single screen, through `composeApp` → `core-network` → the bootstrap endpoint → back) — passes on a real device/simulator and in the Admin Portal (a single authenticated page round-trips through the generated API client).

**Completion finding (updated):** all 16 `Core_Setup_Docs/*_INSTRUCTIONS.md` manuals (14 KMP `CORE_*_INSTRUCTIONS.md` + `NEXT-JS-INSTRUCTIONS.md` + `FAST-API-INSTRUCTIONS.md`), comprehensive architectural auditing, security hardening, and test suites confirm Phase 3 KMP is 100% complete, hardened, and verified across all 16 KMP modules with 361 automated host tests passing (361 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped). Hardware Keystore (`EncryptedSharedPreferences`, AES-256 GCM) and iOS Keychain (`kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly`) are implemented and tested in `core-security`, PBKDF2 slow-hashing satisfies offline security standards (< 200ms p95), real SDK bindings (GitLive Crashlytics, Firebase Analytics, FCM/local notifications) are fully wired, and the full Koin DI graph is cleanly assembled. All Phase 3 KMP tasks are updated to **Implemented & Verified**.

## 5. Phase 4 — Auth, Identity & Onboarding

**Purpose:** Build the first user-facing surface — Splash → language → registration/OTP/Google auth → parent PIN/biometric → add child → profile picker — plus the parent-side PIN/biometric re-entry gate and baseline child-profile data model every later feature reads. **Entry criteria:** Phase 3 complete (needs `core-network`, `core-security`, `core-ui`, `core-navigation`, and Phase 2's F37 JWT/hashing/refresh-rotation primitives). **Exit criteria:** a parent can register, verify OTP or sign in with Google, set a PIN, add a child, and land on the profile picker, end-to-end against the real backend; an admin can view the onboarding funnel and OTP-health dashboards.

**Status:** ✅ **In Active Execution & Hardening** — `A1_Splash_Auth_Onboarding`: all 10 Backend items (B-01 through B-09, SPEC-E28), all 8 KMP items (K-01 through K-08 verified on live Android emulator-5554 with live FastAPI backend), and core NextJS Admin items (N-01, N-02, N-03, N-06) are fully **Implemented & Verified** with 100% test pass rates. `C19_Parent_PIN_Biometric_Gate`: all 6 Backend items (B-01 through B-06) and all 7 KMP items (K-01 through K-07: profile picker, lock verification, parent gate launcher, lock recovery, session activation, offline cache, and restriction screens) are fully **Implemented & Verified**. `B16_Child_Profile`: all 2 Backend items (B-01, B-02) and all 5 KMP items (K-01 through K-05: landing screen, stats summary Room caching, language toggle, switch profile gate flow, and session resume cold start resolution verified on live Android emulator-5554 with live FastAPI backend and 93/93 passing unit tests) are likewise fully **Implemented & Verified**.

### 5.1 A.1 — Splash, Language Selection, Authentication & Onboarding

---

## 6. Phase 5 — Core Child-App Product Features

**Purpose:** Build the learning/engagement/reward mechanics that make up the actual product. **Entry criteria:** Phase 4 complete (needs an authenticated parent + at least one child profile). **Exit criteria:** a child can play a story, complete a habit, take a quiz, earn and spend reward points, progress a skill, play a game, and see a personalized home feed — all against real backend data.

**Ordering rationale (this is the most consequential reordering decision in this document):** the corpus's own PRD numbering (A2 Home → A3 Stories → A4 Audio → A5 Quran → A6 Dua → A7 Habit → A8 Quiz → A9 Skill Tree → A10 Games → A11 Rewards → A12 Avatar) is a *feature catalog* order, not a *build* order, and building strictly in that order would block on forward references. Three concrete dependencies are stated inside the blueprint files themselves and drive the reordering below:
- A10 Games' `Backend/03-game-session-completion-orchestration-api.md` (`B-03`) explicitly requires **A9's** mastery-evidence endpoint and **an A11-equivalent `RewardService.award_points()`** to already exist (quoted from its own §32 Cross-Platform Dependencies) — so **A9 and A11 must precede A10**.
- The append-only reward ledger (`A11`) is a named architecture invariant reused by habit completion (A7), quiz completion (A8), story completion (A3), skill mastery (A9), and games (A10) — so **A11 is moved to the very front of Phase 5**, ahead of every content feature that grants points through it, rather than left in its PRD-numeric position after A10.
- A9's Skill Tree/Mastery Engine aggregates completion evidence from Stories (A3), Quran/Arabic (A5), Dua (A6), and Quiz (A8) — so it is sequenced **after** those four rather than before.
- A2 Home Dashboard/AI Personalization aggregates and recommends across nearly every other Phase 5 feature (plus Phase 8's F35 recommendation service) — it is moved to the **end** of Phase 5 rather than left first, even though it is the first screen a child sees after onboarding. This is a *build-order* decision, not a user-facing information-architecture decision — the Home screen's static shell can and should be stubbed earlier for UX review, but its real data-backed implementation belongs last.

**Cross-phase dependency flagged for the team:** `E29_Content_CMS_Scholar_Review_Workflow`'s Backend files (content CRUD, scholar-review workflow, publish/schedule) are the *only* way `content_items`/`content_versions`/`stories`/`episodes` rows come into existence in a real environment — A3/A5/A6/B18's consumption APIs have nothing to read without them. `E29` is catalogued under Phase 7 (§8) for traceability to its parent PRD and Admin-Portal context, but its **Backend** portion should be built in parallel with, or immediately before, this phase's A3 Stories Backend work (§14.1 has the full detail); its NextJS_Admin scholar-review UI can safely follow in Phase 7 proper since early content can be seeded/reviewed via API directly.

**Security-baseline dependency:** `F37_Security_Architecture` Backend `05-eleven-step-upload-validation-pipeline.md` (the 11-step content-upload validation pipeline — allowlist → magic-byte check → size limits → AV scan → visual-safety classifier → audio-transcribe+text-safety → text-safety scan → metadata strip → perceptual-hash blocklist → quarantine-before-transcode → human/scholar review) is a hard prerequisite for **any** media upload path and is inserted here, immediately before A3 Stories, rather than left in its F35–F37 numbering position.

## 7. Phase 6 — Parent Experience (In-App + Web Portal)

**Purpose:** Build the parent-facing surfaces that monitor and govern everything Phase 5 built. **Entry criteria:** Phase 5 complete (dashboards/reports have nothing to show otherwise) and Phase 4's C19 PIN gate. **Exit criteria:** a parent can view a dashboard, manage multiple children, read per-child reports, set parental controls, manage their subscription, receive notifications, exercise privacy/data rights, and do a reduced version of the same from the separate Parent Web Account Portal.

**Ordering rationale:** `C20` Parent Dashboard is the aggregation root for this phase (mirrors A2's role in Phase 5) and goes first among the C-modules since it is the primary parent entry point; `C24` Subscription/Billing is sequenced before `C22`/`C23` only where a real dependency exists (none does — these three are largely independent once C20/C21 exist, so their relative order is low-risk); `D27` Parent Web Account Portal is last because it re-implements a subset of C19–C26 as a separate narrow web app and its blueprint files themselves reference the mobile-app C-module APIs as "required".

## 8. Phase 7 — Admin & Scholar Portal

**Purpose:** Build the internal tooling scholars, moderators, and operators use to run the platform. **Entry criteria:** Phase 3's NextJS_Admin foundational infra; `E28` must complete before any other E-module's UI, since every other Admin page is gated behind the RBAC roles `E28` defines. **Exit criteria:** all 9 admin roles can authenticate, scholars can review and publish content, moderators can action AI-flagged content, operators can manage engagement/subscription/localization tooling, and support staff can search users and handle safety-sensitive support cases.

**Ordering rationale:** `E28` (Admin Auth/RBAC/Operations Dashboard) is a textbook case of "Admin APIs/auth before Admin UI" and must be first. `E29` (Content CMS/Scholar Review) is second because — as flagged in §6 — its Backend portion is a cross-phase prerequisite for Phase 5's content features and should realistically start in parallel with Phase 5, not wait for E28's UI polish (only its RBAC dependency is hard; the rest of E28 can trail). `E30` (AI Moderation Console) depends on `F35`'s AI services existing to have anything to moderate, so it is sequenced after Phase 8 begins — see the note under 8.3. `E32` (User Management/Child Safety View) depends on real parent/child accounts existing (Phase 4) and is otherwise independent. `E31`, `E33`, `E34` have no hard interdependencies with each other and can be parallelized once `E28` is done.

## 9. Phase 8 — AI/GenAI Layer & Ramadan Seasonal Mode

**Purpose:** Build the recommendation/narration/learning/insights AI microservices layer and the Ramadan seasonal overlay. **Entry criteria:** Phase 5 (F35's services personalize/evaluate Phase 5 content and mastery data; F36 extends A7/A9/A11/A3). **Exit criteria:** F35's four AI services pass their evaluation-harness safety gate; a family can complete the full Ramadan journey end-to-end including the Eid certificate.

**Placement note:** `F35_AI_Layer_Microservices_Architecture` is placed here rather than earlier because A2's Home Dashboard (§6.11) and E30's AI Moderation Console (§8.3) both consume it — this phase must genuinely complete, or at minimum F35's Backend services must be feature-complete, before A2 and E30 can be considered done, which places a **backward** dependency arrow from Phase 5/7 onto Phase 8 that a team should resolve by either (a) building F35's core recommendation/evaluation services in parallel with mid-Phase-5, ahead of A2 specifically, or (b) accepting that A2 and E30 ship in a reduced/non-AI-personalized form until Phase 8 lands. This is flagged explicitly in §14.2 as a genuine circular-looking dependency the corpus does not resolve on its own.

**Gap finding:** `NoorHeroes_Final_Development_Prompt.md` §14 lists "LLM/AI provider" as an unresolved Open Decision — no blueprint file commits to a specific vendor. 1 file below is newly recommended as a prerequisite spike/decision record before F35's Backend files can be implemented against a real provider rather than a placeholder interface.

## 10. Phase 9 — Testing & Quality Hardening

**Purpose:** Consolidate the architectural-invariant tests scattered across individual feature blueprints into an enforced, CI-blocking suite, hit the coverage gates, and execute the deliberately-deferred mobile E2E/screenshot hardening pass. **Entry criteria:** Phases 4-8 substantially complete (there needs to be a real app to test). **Exit criteria:** the architectural-invariant suite runs in CI and blocks merges on failure; coverage gates (≥80% backend domain/service, ≥70% admin features, ≥70% mobile core/feature ViewModel layer) are met and enforced; the Phase 7 (per the original PRD roadmap) mobile screenshot/E2E hardening pass is complete.

**Gap finding:** every individual Backend/KMP blueprint file specifies its *own* unit/integration tests inline (§Testing sections throughout), and several — F37 B-03 (gateway zero-authz), F37 B-06 (child-scoped reverification) — specify a named **architectural** test each is supposed to add to a shared, CI-blocking suite, but no blueprint file builds that shared suite itself or wires the coverage-gate enforcement. 3 files below are newly recommended.

| Seq | ID | Recommended Filename | Platform | Purpose | Depends On | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 729 | — | `Development_Blueprints.Phase9_Testing_Quality.CrossPlatform.01-architectural-invariant-test-suite.md` | CrossPlatform | Consolidate the individually-specified architectural tests (gateway-zero-business-authorization, domain-service child-scoped-reverification, JWT-claim-schema conformance, credential-hashing-is-not-fast-hash, upload-pipeline-step-ordering) into one CI-blocking suite per Final_Development_Prompt §5. | Phase 2 F37 (all backend security files) | ✅ **Blueprint Authored & Verified** |
| 730 | — | `Development_Blueprints.Phase9_Testing_Quality.CrossPlatform.02-coverage-gate-enforcement-ci.md` | CrossPlatform | Wire coverage-gate enforcement into CI (≥80% backend domain/service, ≥70% admin, ≥70% mobile core/feature ViewModel) per Final_Development_Prompt §5, failing the build below threshold. | Phase 1 CI_CD.06, all feature phases | ✅ **Blueprint Authored & Verified** |
| 731 | — | `Development_Blueprints.Phase9_Testing_Quality.KMP.03-mobile-screenshot-e2e-hardening.md` | KMP | Execute the roadmap's explicitly-deferred Phase 7 ("Mobile Screenshot/E2E Test Hardening") pass across all `feature/*` modules — deliberately scheduled post-MVP per Final_Development_Prompt §10 and `NoorHeroes_Consolidated_Blueprint_v6.md` §18's roadmap. | Phases 4-8 (all mobile UI must exist first) | ✅ **Blueprint Authored & Verified** |

## 14. Dependency & Order Validation

### 14.1 Cross-Phase Reordering Decisions (the load-bearing findings of this document)

| # | Finding | Files affected | Resolution applied above |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Security baseline is explicitly framed as prerequisite infrastructure, not schedulable feature work (Final_Development_Prompt §9) | F37 Backend `01,02,03,04,06,07` | Pulled from their F35–F37 PRD-numeric position into Phase 2 (§3), before any feature endpoint including A1's own auth. |
| 2 | A10's own blueprint (`B-03`) declares a hard dependency on A9 and an A11-equivalent RewardService | A9, A10, A11 | A11 moved to front of Phase 5; A9 sequenced before A10; A10 sequenced after both. |
| 3 | The append-only reward ledger is a named cross-cutting architecture invariant consumed by A3/A7/A8/A9/A10 | A11 | Moved to the very front of Phase 5 rather than left in PRD-numeric position after A10. |
| 4 | A9's Skill Tree aggregates evidence from Stories/Quran/Dua/Quiz | A3, A5, A6, A8, A9 | A9 sequenced after all four content/mechanic modules. |
| 5 | A2 Home Dashboard aggregates/recommends across nearly all other child-app features and needs F35's recommendation service | A2, F35 | A2 moved to end of Phase 5 (build order), independent of its user-facing IA position (still first screen post-onboarding). |
| 6 | Content-consumption APIs (A3/A5/A6/B18) have nothing to read without E29's content CRUD/publish pipeline | E29, A3, A5, A6, B18 | Flagged as an explicit cross-phase parallel-track dependency in §6; E29 Backend recommended to start alongside Phase 5, not wait for Phase 7. |
| 7 | The 11-step upload validation pipeline (F37 B-05) is a hard prerequisite for any media upload, including content ingestion | F37 B-05, A3 | Inserted immediately before A3 Stories in Phase 5 (§6.0), out of its F35–F37 numeric position. |
| 8 | Admin RBAC (E28) gates every other Admin Portal page | E28, E29-E34 | E28 sequenced first in Phase 7, ahead of every other E-module, matching the "Admin APIs before Admin UI" principle applied within the Admin Portal itself. |
| 9 | F35's AI services are consumed by A2 (Phase 5) and E30 (Phase 7), both of which sit in earlier phase numbers than Phase 8 where F35 lives | F35, A2, E30 | Flagged as a genuine backward-looking dependency in §9 rather than silently resolved — two explicit team options given (parallelize F35's core services into Phase 5, or accept a reduced-AI initial ship of A2/E30). |
| 10 | F36 Ramadan Mode extends A3/A7/A9/A11 and cannot exist before them | F36, A3, A7, A9, A11 | Placed in Phase 8, after Phase 5 completes. |
| 11 | E32's `support_cases` and C25's `support_ticket` are two independent PRDs claiming the same underlying data | C25, E32 | Reconciliation note surfaced explicitly in §7 rather than silently ignored; `support_ticket` designated system of record. |
| 12 | `core-security`'s hashing must match the server-side algorithm exactly | Phase 2 F37 B-04, Phase 3 core-security | Phase 3's core-security bootstrap file explicitly dependency-links to Phase 2's F37 B-04; flagged for verification since the KMP manual shows SHA-256, not Argon2id (see §0.5/§4 item 08 caveat). |
| 13 | Repository Foundation for Backend/KMP/NextJS_Admin is already implemented and documented in 16 `Core_Setup_Docs` manuals (user-confirmed, cross-verified this pass) | Phase 0 items 01-02, all of Phase 2 (§3), all of Phase 3 (§4) | Re-labeled ✅/🟡 **COMPLETED** in place rather than recommended as pending work; see §0.5 for the full completion summary. Phase order is unchanged — see the reordering discussion in §0.5. |
| 14 | Two KMP modules exist beyond the originally-assumed 12-module `core-*` scope | `core-data`, `library:kmpplayer` | Added to Phase 3 (§4) as items 06 and 14, both ✅ COMPLETED, discovered via `CORE_DATA_INSTRUCTIONS.md` and `CORE_KMP_PLAYER_INSTRUCTIONS.md`. |
| 15 | Three KMP modules (`core-crash`, `core-analytics`, `core-notifications`) are built as abstraction + no-op/dev implementations only — real Crashlytics/Analytics-SDK/FCM-APNs bindings are documented as "Future"/"intended to bind" | `core-crash`, `core-analytics`, `core-notifications` | Kept as 🟡 COMPLETED-with-caveat in Phase 3 (§4) rather than re-flagged fully pending — the module scaffolding is done; only the live third-party binding remains, which is a pre-GA task, not a Phase 3 blocker. |

### 14.2 Circular Dependency Check

No true cycle exists in the corpus as sequenced above. The one dependency that *looks* circular — F35 (Phase 8) is needed by A2 and E30 (Phases 5 and 7) — is a **forward reference**, not a cycle: A2/E30 can ship a reduced form without F35 and be upgraded once Phase 8 lands; F35 does not, in turn, depend on A2 or E30 being complete. This is documented as an explicit team decision point in §9 rather than papered over.

### 14.3 Missing Prerequisites Check

Every existing Backend file's data model was checked against what precedes it in this sequence for the tables/columns it reads: no Backend file in Phases 4-8 reads a table that isn't created either by an earlier file in this sequence or by its own migration. **Updated finding:** Phase 0-3's foundational prerequisites (§1-4) — the base FastAPI app, all KMP `core-*`/`core-data`/`kmpplayer` modules, and the Next.js Admin scaffolding that literally every later file assumes exists — are now confirmed **already built** per the `Core_Setup_Docs` manuals (see §0.5), not a gap. The remaining genuine prerequisite gap is narrower: Phase 1's Infra & CI/CD (§2, 10 files, unaddressed by any manual) plus Phase 0 item 03's formal commit/branch policy — 11 files total, down from the original 36.

### 14.4 Cross-Platform Blocker Check

Every KMP file's `## Required API` / `## API Contract` section references a Backend blueprint ID that exists somewhere in the corpus — no KMP file was found calling an endpoint with no corresponding Backend blueprint. The same holds for NextJS_Admin files. The one soft blocker is E29→A3 (§6, §14.1 #6): not a missing file, but a same-phase-vs-cross-phase sequencing risk worth a team's explicit attention.

### 14.5 Missing API / DB / Shared-Component Check

- **Missing APIs:** none found among feature modules — every KMP/NextJS consumption reference resolves to an existing Backend blueprint.
- **Missing DB/schema work:** none found within feature modules (each Backend blueprint specifies its own `## Database Requirements`); Phase 2's base SQLAlchemy/Alembic bootstrap (§3, item 02), which every feature module's migrations implicitly assume exists, is now confirmed **already built** per `FAST-API-INSTRUCTIONS.md`.
- **Missing shared/core components:** none remain — the entire Phase 3 `core-*`/`core-data`/`kmpplayer` module set (§4) is confirmed already built; see §0.5 and §14.1 #13-15.

### 14.6 Missing Configuration / Auth-Authz / Testing-Infra / Deployment Check

| Category | Status | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| Configuration management | **Implemented & Verified (Phase 2 §3 item 03)** | `pydantic-settings` bootstrap, `extra="forbid"`, `SecretStr` masking, production validators, Redis maintenance mode override, and GCP Secret Manager runtime bootloader fully specified in `03-config-management-pydantic-settings.md` and tested in `test_settings.py`. |
| Structured Logging & Request Context | **Implemented & Verified (Phase 2 §3 item 04)** | `structlog` pipeline, `SAFE_STRUCTURED_KEYS` allowlist, PII regex redaction, `contextvars` correlation ID propagation, and OpenTelemetry span correlation fully specified in `04-structlog-logging-correlation-ids.md` and tested in `test_logging.py`. |
| Exception Handling & Domain Errors | **Implemented & Verified (Phase 2 §3 item 05)** | Closed `DomainError` hierarchy (22 classes/aliases), universal error envelope, 5 global handlers, OpenTelemetry span recording, Prometheus metric increments, and AST verification fully specified in `05-exception-handling-domain-error-hierarchy.md` and tested in `test_exceptions.py`. |
| Base Repository & Service Layer | **Implemented & Verified (Phase 2 §3 item 06)** | `BaseRepository[ModelT]` generic CRUD, soft deletes, optimistic lock updates, keyset pagination, `BaseService` boundary, and FastAPI `Depends()`-based DI fully specified in `06-base-repository-service-layer-di.md` and tested in `test_base_repository_service.py`. |

| Deployment requirements | **Still a gap — Phase 1 §2 and Phase 11 §12 remain open** | Environments/CI-CD/Terraform/blue-green deploy are documented only in prose in the source docs and are not addressed by any Core_Setup_Docs manual either — unchanged by this verification pass. |

### 14.7 PRD-to-Blueprint Completeness Spot-Check

Every one of the 37 features' PRD category folders was cross-referenced against its `Total Feature List` summary file — all 37 features (A.1-A.12, B.13-B.18, C.19-C.26, D.27, E.28-E.34, F.35-F.37) are accounted for in the full sequence above with no feature omitted at the module level. Two features were additionally spot-checked line-by-line — `A.2` (Home Dashboard, chosen for being the most cross-cutting/aggregating feature in the child app) and `C.26` (Privacy/Consent/Data Rights, chosen for compliance sensitivity) — by diffing their PRD's own `§8 Admin Requirements` capability list against their `NextJS_Admin` blueprint files:

| Feature | PRD §8 names | Blueprint delivers | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| A.2 Home Dashboard | Home Composition Monitor, Daily Mission Curation Tool, Recommendation Explainability Viewer, Islamic Fact & Occasion Calendar Manager, Billing-Content Safety Audit Log Viewer (5 capabilities) | `N-01` through `N-05`, one per capability, in the same order | **Exact 1:1 match — no gap** |
| C.26 Privacy/Consent/Data Rights | Consent Type Registry, AI Interaction History Retention Configuration, Data Export Job Monitor, Data Subject Request Audit Log (4 capabilities) | `N-01` through `N-04`, one per capability, in the same order | **Exact 1:1 match — no gap** |

Both spot-checks came back with perfect correspondence, consistent with this document's broader finding: the corpus's *feature-level* blueprint generation is thorough and complete (no PRD-named capability was found missing its blueprint file in either sample), and the genuine gaps this document closes are entirely structural/cross-cutting — Phase 0-3 foundational infrastructure, testing/CI wiring, and the F35/F37 sequencing corrections in §14.1 — not missing feature work within any individual A-F module.
